And suddenly I'm right back there. I can actually smell the smoke, and I see my brother. It's like the real world, the job site, just drops out from under me. Or even the people living under my roof. I just don't trust myself around anyone anymore.\n\nDoctor: Did your brother die in an accident at work?\n\nPatient: I... I really can't get into the details of exactly what happened right now, if that's okay. It's just too hard to talk about. I just know he's gone.\n\nDoctor: Were you there when your brother died?\n\nPatient: I... look, I'd really rather not answer that. Like I said, I just can't talk about the details of that day. I'm sorry, it's just... it's too much right now.\n\nDoctor: Do unwanted memories of what happened with your brother keep popping into your mind even when you try not to think about it?\n\nPatient: Yeah. Yeah, they do. It happens when I'm not even trying to think about it. I'll just be in the middle of wiring a panel, or... or even just hearing my nephew shut the garage door at home. And suddenly I'm right back there. I can actually smell the smoke, and I see my brother. It's like the real world, the job site, just drops out from under me. I try to keep my mind on my work, but it just comes out of nowhere.\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding reminders of what happened to your brother?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. I mean, I don't even go in my own garage anymore unless I absolutely have to. I just steer clear of it. I also quit helping out with the electrical stuff at my church. And if people start talking about... you know, car projects, or the fire... I just shut them down fast. I just can't be around any of that stuff right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling constantly on guard or easily startled since your brother died?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I don't think I'm any more jumpy than usual. I mostly just try to stay away from the stuff that reminds me of it, like I was saying. But as far as feeling on guard all the time or being easily startled... no, I haven't really noticed that happening.\n\nDoctor: Since your brother died, have you been blaming yourself for what happened?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. Honestly, that's a big part of why I came in here to see you. I just... I can't shake the thought that if all my years as an electrician couldn't keep my own brother safe, then I'm a danger to anybody who works with me. Or even the people living under my own roof. I just keep thinking it's my fault.\n\nDoctor: Since your brother died, have you been feeling down or depressed most days?\n\nPatient: Yeah, pretty much every day. It's just... there's this heavy mix of guilt and anger that just sits in my chest all day long. It's especially bad when I look at my nephew. I just see him and think about how he lost his dad, and now he got stuck with me instead. So, yeah, I've definitely been feeling pretty down.\n\nDoctor: Have you had thoughts of killing yourself?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. I mean... as bad as I feel, and as much as I blame myself, I haven't thought about actually ending my life. I've got my nephew to look after now, so... no, I wouldn't do that.\n\nDoctor: How long has this been going on since your brother died?\n\nPatient: It's been about four months now. Basically ever since the garage fire happened... and my nephew came to live with me. That's when all this really started.\n\nDoctor: When those memories hit, does it ever feel like you’re actually back in that moment, as if it’s happening again right now?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it really does. I'll be in the middle of wiring a panel at work, or... or I'll just hear my nephew shut the garage door at home, and suddenly I'm right back there. It's like the job site just drops out from under me. I can actually smell the smoke, and... I can see my brother. It feels exactly like it's happening all over again right in that second.\n\nDoctor: Were you there when the garage fire happened?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I was. I was right there when it happened.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. But as far as feeling on guard all the time or being easily startled... no, I haven't really noticed that happening.\n\nDoctor: Since your brother died, have you been blaming yourself for what happened?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. Honestly, that's a big part of why I came in here to see you. I just... I can't shake the thought that if all my years as an electrician couldn't keep my own brother safe, then I'm a danger to anybody who works with me. Or even the people living under my own roof. I just keep thinking it's my fault.\n\nDoctor: Since your brother died, have you been feeling down or depressed most days?\n\nPatient: Yeah, pretty much every day. It's just... there's this heavy mix of guilt and anger that just sits in my chest all day long. It's especially bad when I look at my nephew. I just see him and think about how he lost his dad, and now he got stuck with me instead. So, yeah, I've definitely been feeling pretty down.\n\nDoctor: Have you had thoughts of killing yourself?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. I mean... as bad as I feel, and as much as I blame myself, I haven't thought about actually ending my life. I've got my nephew to look after now, so... no, I wouldn't do that.\n\nDoctor: How long has this been going on since your brother died?\n\nPatient: It's been about four months now. Basically ever since the garage fire happened... and my nephew came to live with me. That's when all this really started.\n\nDoctor: When those memories hit, does it ever feel like you’re actually back in that moment, as if it’s happening again right now?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it really does. I'll be in the middle of wiring a panel at work, or... or I'll just hear my nephew shut the garage door at home, and suddenly I'm right back there. It's like the job site just drops out from under me. I can actually smell the smoke, and... I can see my brother. It feels exactly like it's happening all over again right in that second.\n\nDoctor: Were you there when the garage fire happened?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I was. I was right there when it happened.\n\nDoctor: Since the fire, have you found yourself believing that you’re unsafe or that you can’t trust yourself around other people?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. Honestly, that's exactly why I came in to see you. I just... I can't shake this thought that if all my years as an electrician couldn't keep my own brother safe, then I must be a danger to anybody who works with me. Or... or anyone who lives under my roof now. I just feel like I can't be trusted to keep them safe.\n\nDoctor: Since the fire, have you been having trouble sleeping?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I have. Most nights I just lie there in the dark, listening for any little sound... like a crackling noise or a car door out in the driveway. And when I finally do manage to fall asleep, I end up jolting awake, just completely soaked in sweat from dreaming about the garage. Once that happens, I can't get back to sleep before my alarm goes off for work.\n\nDoctor: Since your brother died, have you lost interest or pleasure in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I still try to do the things I usually do. I still watch the game on Sundays, and I still try to get out and fish when the weather's decent. I wouldn't say I've lost interest in that stuff. It just feels... normal, I guess. I haven't noticed any change there.\n\nDoctor: Since the fire, have you been feeling constantly on guard or watchful, like you have to stay alert for danger?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say that. I mean... aside from what I mentioned about nighttime, just lying awake listening for noises, I don't really feel like that during the day. When I'm at work or just out doing things, I'm not looking over my shoulder or waiting for something bad to happen. So, no, I haven't noticed feeling constantly on guard.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having nightmares about the fire?\n\nPatient: Not exactly. I mean, like I mentioned a minute ago, I do have those dreams about the garage that wake me up in a sweat... but I don't actually see the fire or the flames in them. It's just the garage. So, no, I wouldn't say I'm having nightmares about the actual fire.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing.
